--- CHAPTER RESEARCH 7B ---

**Orientation**
Lula’s personal-integrity record in 2023 is mixed in an important but bounded way: the year did not show a new corruption scandal centered on Lula personally, nor evidence of family self-dealing in the sources inspected here, but it did show a long anti-corruption legacy being actively re-litigated in court and politics. The strongest evidence is about institutional integrity around him: the STF’s 2023 actions against previous investigations, the TCU’s reservations on 2023 accounts, and open public scrutiny of platform and election issues. For Lula personally, the record is strongest on truthful public positioning in some policy areas, but there are also clear cases where rhetoric shifted for political advantage, especially when he framed economic and diplomatic compromises as victories or necessity. The main caution is that “no evidence found” is not proof of clean personal conduct; it is only the limit of the inspected record.

**Compact evidence index**
- No inspected 2023 source shows Lula or close family extracting personal financial gain from office.
- The 2023 record does show active courts, auditors, and prosecutors capable of investigating powerful actors, which supports independent accountability.
- Lula’s 2023 public statements were often candid about compromise, but also strategically framed political defeats as victories.
- There is no inspected evidence of nepotistic family appointments or Lula using office to block investigation.
- The strongest contrary context is that Lula’s prior corruption history remained a live political issue, but that is mostly retrospective context rather than 2023 proof of new misconduct.

**Lens disposition**
- **7B.1 Truthfulness:** Partially supported.
- **7B.10 Ethical example and public trust:** Mixed.
- **7B.2 Correcting falsehoods and errors:** Limited evidence.
- **7B.3 Conflicts of interest:** No strong evidence of violation; limited positive evidence of restraint.
- **7B.4 Personal profit from office:** No usable 2023 evidence of improper personal profit.
- **7B.5 Nepotism and loyalist appointments:** Limited evidence.
- **7B.6 Independent investigation:** Supported.
- **7B.7 Obstruction and concealment:** No strong 2023 evidence of obstruction by Lula.
- **7B.8 Promises and good faith:** Partially supported.
- **7B.9 Favoritism and clientelism:** Limited evidence.

**Evidence records**

1. **No 2023 evidence of personal financial self-dealing found in the inspected record**
- **Claim:** In the sources inspected for 2023, there was no credible evidence that Lula or his close family directly profited from office through contracts, gifts, bribes, foundations, insider access, or asset concealment. This matters because it is the core negative finding for the personal-profit lens.
- **Source:** Synthesis from the inspected 2023 record set.
- **Locator:** N/A.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Negative evidence from the inspected record.
- **Contrary evidence:** Lula’s earlier corruption history remained politically salient, but no 2023 source here established new personal profiteering.
- **Cautions:** Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence; this is limited to the inspected sources.
- **Corroboration:** TCU, STF, and prosecutor/court activity show the broader accountability environment.
- **Supports:** 7B.4, 7B.7, 7B.9, 7B.10

2. **The STF and prosecutors remained able to investigate powerful actors**
- **Claim:** In 2023, the STF opened and expanded January 8 investigations, and TSE actions showed that high-level political actors could still be sanctioned. This matters because independent investigation is a prerequisite for integrity accountability.
- **Source:** STF, 2023-01-26, *A pedido da PGR, STF abre três inquéritos e amplia investigação sobre atos terroristas de 8/1*. [Link](https://portal.stf.jus.br/noticias/verNoticiaDetalhe.asp?idConteudo=500967&ori=1)
- **Locator:** Opening paragraphs.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Institutional independence under Lula’s presidency.
- **Contrary evidence:** This does not prove Lula welcomed every investigation of his own circle.
- **Cautions:** Court-news summary, not a full case file.
- **Corroboration:** TSE ineligibility ruling and TCU account review.
- **Supports:** 7B.6, 7B.7, 7B.10

3. **The TCU scrutinized Lula’s 2023 accounts and found reservations**
- **Claim:** The TCU approved Lula’s 2023 accounts with reservations, identifying tax waivers, transparency issues, and other problems. This matters because it shows independent fiscal scrutiny remained active rather than being captured or silenced.
- **Source:** TCU, 2024-06-12, *TCU aprova com ressalvas as contas do presidente da República de 2023*. [Link](https://portal.tcu.gov.br/imprensa/noticias/tcu-aprova-com-ressalvas-as-contas-do-presidente-da-republica-de-2023.htm)
- **Locator:** Lines 54-58 and 62-80.
- **Period:** Later retrospective on 2023.
- **Attribution basis:** Independent audit of Lula’s administration.
- **Contrary evidence:** The approval was not clean; the reservations matter.
- **Cautions:** Audit findings concern fiscal governance more than personal ethics directly.
- **Corroboration:** STF and prosecutor scrutiny.
- **Supports:** 7B.6, 7B.7, 7B.10

4. **Lula publicly framed tax reform as a political compromise, not a pure technocratic ideal**
- **Claim:** Lula said the tax reform text was the “possible” one, not what Haddad wanted, and later called approval a “great victory.” This matters because it shows he was willing to present compromise as success, which can be honest but also opportunistic.
- **Source:** Reuters via UOL, 2023-07-06, *Texto da reforma tributária não é o desejado por Haddad, mas o possível, diz Lula*. [Link](https://economia.uol.com.br/noticias/reuters/2023/07/06/texto-da-reforma-tributaria-nao-e-o-desejado-por-haddad-mas-o-possivel-diz-lula.htm)
- **Locator:** Lead paragraphs and quoted remarks.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Direct presidential rhetoric.
- **Contrary evidence:** Calling a compromise “the possible” text is candid rather than deceptive; the main issue is political framing.
- **Cautions:** This is a political quote, not a sworn statement.
- **Corroboration:** Reuters on approval and later congressional promulgation.
- **Supports:** 7B.1, 7B.2, 7B.8, 7B.10

5. **Lula publicly credited Haddad and the economic team**
- **Claim:** Lula repeatedly praised Finance Minister Fernando Haddad and described the economic team as functioning well. This matters because it suggests delegation to qualified professionals rather than purely loyalist management.
- **Source:** Reuters via UOL, 2023-04-10 and 2023-07-06. [Link](https://economia.uol.com.br/noticias/reuters/2023/04/10/lula-volta-a-criticar-taxa-de-juros-e-diz-que-arcabouco-fiscal-deve-ser-aprovado-no-congresso.htm)
- **Locator:** Lead paragraphs and Lula’s quoted remarks about Haddad.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Personnel choice and public endorsement.
- **Contrary evidence:** Praise does not itself prove independence or honesty; it can be strategic.
- **Cautions:** Political rhetoric; no direct personnel dossier.
- **Corroboration:** Fiscal-framework law and TCU review.
- **Supports:** 7B.5, 7B.8, 7B.10

6. **Bolsonaro-era secrecy and transparency were reversed at the start of Lula’s government**
- **Claim:** Lula’s government moved to reverse secrecy practices associated with Bolsonaro, signaling a public commitment to transparency rather than concealment. This matters because integrity depends on access and disclosure norms.
- **Source:** federal transparency policy reporting, 2023. [Link](https://www.gov.br/acessoainformacao/pt-br/noticias/governo-federal-tera-politica-de-transparencia-e-acesso-a-informacao)
- **Locator:** Policy announcement page.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Direct executive choice.
- **Contrary evidence:** Transparency reform is not the same as clean conduct by individuals.
- **Cautions:** Official self-description.
- **Corroboration:** STF/TCU oversight environment.
- **Supports:** 7B.6, 7B.7, 7B.10

7. **No evidence Lula obstructed major accountability institutions in 2023**
- **Claim:** The 2023 record inspected here does not show Lula using vetoes, decrees, dismissals, or secrecy to block courts, auditors, or prosecutors from investigating him or his administration. This matters because obstruction is a central integrity test.
- **Source:** Synthesis from the inspected record.
- **Locator:** N/A.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Negative evidence from the source set.
- **Contrary evidence:** Negative evidence cannot exclude hidden obstruction not surfaced by the inspected sources.
- **Cautions:** Limited to the record reviewed.
- **Corroboration:** STF and TCU actions proceeded publicly.
- **Supports:** 7B.6, 7B.7, 7B.10

8. **Lula’s 2023 rhetoric often emphasized compromise and coalition reality**
- **Claim:** Lula openly described the need to govern with Congress and to accept the “possible” rather than the ideal in tax reform. This matters because it is evidence of political candor, though also of tactical framing.
- **Source:** Reuters via UOL, 2023-06-02 and 2023-07-06. [Link](https://economia.uol.com.br/noticias/reuters/2023/06/02/lula-diz-que-e-preciso-conversar-com-que-nao-gosta-do-governo-porque-esquerda-e-minoria-no-congresso.htm), [Link](https://economia.uol.com.br/noticias/reuters/2023/07/06/texto-da-reforma-tributaria-nao-e-o-desejado-por-haddad-mas-o-possivel-diz-lula.htm)
- **Locator:** Lead paragraphs and quotes.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Direct rhetoric.
- **Contrary evidence:** Strategic framing can still omit inconvenient tradeoffs.
- **Cautions:** Political speech, not sworn testimony.
- **Corroboration:** Congressional and fiscal records.
- **Supports:** 7B.1, 7B.2, 7B.8, 7B.10

9. **Lula’s prior corruption history remained politically salient but is mostly retrospective**
- **Claim:** Books and essays on Lula’s career remain critical of his history and politics, but the 2023 inspected material here does not establish a new personal-honesty scandal. This matters as context, but it should not be overused as direct 2023 evidence.
- **Source:** Secondary biographies and critical syntheses already opened in the reconnaissance set.
- **Locator:** N/A in the 2023 period.
- **Period:** Broader retrospective context.
- **Attribution basis:** Historical context.
- **Contrary evidence:** Historical criticism does not prove current-year misconduct.
- **Cautions:** Use as background only.
- **Corroboration:** None required for the contextual point.
- **Supports:** 7B.10

10. **Appointments evidence is thin**
- **Claim:** The inspected sources do not show a 2023 pattern of family, friendship, donation, or business-network appointments inside the economic or accountability apparatus that would amount to clear nepotism or loyalty capture. This matters because nepotistic appointment patterns are a major personal-integrity test.
- **Source:** Synthesis from the inspected record.
- **Locator:** N/A.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Negative evidence from the source set.
- **Contrary evidence:** The absence of evidence may reflect incomplete personnel coverage.
- **Cautions:** The personnel record is incomplete for this lens.
- **Corroboration:** Public appointments and independent oversight continuing.
- **Supports:** 7B.5, 7B.10

11. **No inspected record showed Lula using gifts or office perks for private profit**
- **Claim:** The 2023 sources inspected here did not reveal accepted findings of gifts, emoluments, or insider-access profit by Lula or close family. This matters because personal enrichment from office is a core corruption test.
- **Source:** Synthesis from the inspected record.
- **Locator:** N/A.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Negative evidence from the source set.
- **Contrary evidence:** Lack of a surfaced scandal is not a clean bill of health.
- **Cautions:** Negative and incomplete.
- **Corroboration:** TCU, STF, and prosecutor activity imply scrutiny existed.
- **Supports:** 7B.4, 7B.7, 7B.9, 7B.10

12. **Public trust was constrained by long-running corruption context**
- **Claim:** Lula’s 2023 integrity environment was shaped by a long post-Lava Jato distrust context and by continued public attention to corruption and political ethics. This matters because public trust is not only about fresh misconduct but also about whether the ruler strengthens norms.
- **Source:** critical biographies and syntheses already opened.
- **Locator:** N/A.
- **Period:** Broader context.
- **Attribution basis:** Historical context.
- **Contrary evidence:** Context is not proof of 2023 misconduct.
- **Cautions:** Use only as backdrop.
- **Corroboration:** None needed.
- **Supports:** 7B.10

**Information environment**
- Strong on official accountability institutions and public rhetoric.
- Weaker on private financial tracing, beneficial ownership, gifts, and family-linked procurement.
- Lula’s 2023 honesty record is therefore easier to assess for institutional openness and public candor than for hidden private enrichment.
- The strongest negative finding is not “Lula was proven clean,” but “the inspected 2023 record did not surface a new personal-profit scandal.”

**Questions for deeper research**
- Were there any 2023 asset, tax, gift, or foundation records that should be checked directly for Lula and close family?
- Did any procurement, licensing, or access decisions in 2023 show favoritism toward Lula-connected networks?
- Are there 2023 ethics-body or federal police files not surfaced here that relate to Lula’s close associates?
- Did Lula comply with all disclosure and transparency obligations in his own office?
- What do the full personnel records show for integrity-sensitive appointments, especially in state enterprises and oversight bodies?

**Promising leads still needing inspection**
- Asset declarations and tax filings for Lula and immediate family.
- Federal ethics-body records for 2023.
- Procurement and contract review for Lula-linked networks or donors.
- Any 2023 investigative reporting on gifts, foundations, or family-linked business benefits.
- Personnel files on integrity-sensitive appointments in federal agencies and state firms.

--- CHAPTER RESEARCH 8B ---

**Orientation**
Lula’s 2023 effectiveness record is strongest where the administration had a clear program, legal tools, and a capable economic/policy team: fiscal re-basing, tax reform, social-protection restoration, housing restart, and a participatory planning process. The year produced real institutional output, not just rhetoric: new fiscal rules, a rebuilt Bolsa Família, a new Minha Casa, Minha Vida, a New PAC, and a 2024–2027 PPA that was drafted in 2023 with broad participation. Outcomes moved in the right direction on growth, unemployment, poverty, and household income. The main limits are that some results were still early, some reforms were compromises, and several gains reflected both Lula policy and broader post-pandemic recovery.

**Compact evidence index**
- Lula and his team had a legible governing program: fiscal rebalancing, social reconstruction, housing, planning, and inclusion.
- That program was translated into laws, decrees, and budget architecture in 2023.
- A professional economic team and participatory planning machinery were used, with Simone Tebet and Fernando Haddad as key implementers.
- Implementation was uneven but real: the New PAC, PPA, Bolsa Família, and Minha Casa, Minha Vida all moved from announcement to formal structure.
- Outcomes improved on GDP, unemployment, poverty, and household income, though investment weakened and inequality remained.
- The record shows adaptation and compromise, but also fiscal restraint, coordination, and some durable institutional build-out.

**Lens disposition**
- **8B.1 Clear governing program:** Supported.
- **8B.10 Durable goal achievement:** Partially supported.
- **8B.2 Turning goals into machinery:** Supported.
- **8B.3 Mobilizing the ruling system:** Supported.
- **8B.4 Capable implementers:** Supported.
- **8B.5 Coordination and control:** Supported.
- **8B.6 From plans to practice:** Supported.
- **8B.7 Progress toward chosen goals:** Supported.
- **8B.8 Learning and correction:** Supported.
- **8B.9 Managing crises and resistance:** Supported.

**Evidence records**

1. **Bolsa Família restored as a durable program**
- **Claim:** Lula signed Law 14.601/2023, reinstating Bolsa Família and making it a permanent federal transfer program. This matters because it clearly states a governing priority and creates durable welfare machinery.
- **Source:** Planalto, *Lei nº 14.601, de 19 de junho de 2023*. [Link](https://www.planalto.gov.br/ccivil_03/_ato2023-2026/2023/lei/l14601.htm)
- **Locator:** Article 1 and opening recitals.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Direct sanction of a flagship social-policy program.
- **Contrary evidence:** Program design is not the same as execution quality.
- **Cautions:** Legal text establishes intent and structure, not success.
- **Corroboration:** IBGE poverty and income outcomes.
- **Supports:** 8B.1, 8B.2, 8B.3, 8B.6, 8B.7, 8B.10

2. **Bolsa Família linked to a multi-ministry delivery system**
- **Claim:** The same law tied Bolsa Família to health, education, social assistance, CadÚnico, and federal-state-municipal coordination. This matters because it is not just a cash transfer; it is an implementation network.
- **Source:** same law. [Link](https://www.planalto.gov.br/ccivil_03/_ato2023-2026/2023/lei/l14601.htm)
- **Locator:** Article 3 and the paragraph on objectives and coordination.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Formal policy design under Lula.
- **Contrary evidence:** Coordination on paper does not guarantee uniform local delivery.
- **Cautions:** Design-level evidence only.
- **Corroboration:** PPA and planning documents.
- **Supports:** 8B.2, 8B.4, 8B.5, 8B.6, 8B.10

3. **Minha Casa, Minha Vida was relaunched**
- **Claim:** Lula signed Law 14.620/2023, relaunching Minha Casa, Minha Vida with goals explicitly tied to housing access, urban development, risk reduction, and improved habitability. This matters because it shows a concrete policy conversion from promise to law.
- **Source:** Planalto, *Lei nº 14.620, de 13 de julho de 2023*. [Link](https://www.planalto.gov.br/ccivil_03/_ato2023-2026/2023/lei/l14620.htm)
- **Locator:** Article 1 and Article 2.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Presidential sanction.
- **Contrary evidence:** Housing delivery depends on financing and local execution.
- **Cautions:** Law is a necessary, not sufficient, condition for results.
- **Corroboration:** Ministry of Cities summary page and decree.
- **Supports:** 8B.1, 8B.2, 8B.6, 8B.7, 8B.10

4. **Minha Casa, Minha Vida was operationalized quickly**
- **Claim:** The March 17, 2023 decree regulated the reactivated housing program, showing rapid translation of political intent into operational rules. This matters because it is a concrete implementation step.
- **Source:** Planalto, *Decreto nº 11.439, de 17 de março de 2023*. [Link](https://www.planalto.gov.br/ccivil_03/_ato2023-2026/2023/decreto/d11439.htm)
- **Locator:** Opening recitals and Article 1.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Direct executive action.
- **Contrary evidence:** Regulation alone does not prove housing delivery.
- **Cautions:** Implementation rules, not outcome data.
- **Corroboration:** Ministry of Cities explanation page.
- **Supports:** 8B.2, 8B.3, 8B.5, 8B.6, 8B.8

5. **New PAC created a large investment platform**
- **Claim:** Lula launched the New PAC in August 2023 with R$1.7 trillion in planned investment, nine policy axes, and explicit social and infrastructure goals. This matters because it is the central umbrella program for Lula’s 2023 governing program.
- **Source:** Ministry of Defense, 2023-08-11, *Com novo PAC, Defesa investirá R$ 53 bilhões em tecnologias estratégicas que garantem a soberania nacional*. [Link](https://www.gov.br/defesa/pt-br/ajuste/com-novo-pac-defesa-investira-r-53-bilhoes-em-tecnologias-estrategicas-que-garantem-a-soberania-nacional/)
- **Locator:** Opening paragraphs on the PAC launch and R$1.7 trillion total.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Presidential launch of a cross-government program.
- **Contrary evidence:** Launches do not guarantee execution.
- **Cautions:** Government announcement; use with implementation evidence.
- **Corroboration:** Ministry of Cities PAC page, Susep notice, PAC decrees.
- **Supports:** 8B.1, 8B.2, 8B.3, 8B.5, 8B.6, 8B.10

6. **PAC machinery was institutionalized**
- **Claim:** Decree 11.630/2023 created an interministerial PAC innovation/acquisition commission to steer procurement and technology policy. This matters because it shows the program was given administrative machinery, not left as a slogan.
- **Source:** Planalto, *Decreto nº 11.630, de 11 de agosto de 2023*. [Link](https://www.planalto.gov.br/ccivil_03/_ato2023-2026/2023/decreto/d11630.htm)
- **Locator:** Article 1 and Article 2.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Direct executive institutional design.
- **Contrary evidence:** Institutional creation is not proof of effective procurement.
- **Cautions:** Formal machinery only.
- **Corroboration:** PAC launch and later transfer rules.
- **Supports:** 8B.2, 8B.3, 8B.5, 8B.6, 8B.8

7. **PAC transfers were turned into workable federal instruments**
- **Claim:** Decree 11.855/2023 set rules for compulsory federal transfers to states and municipalities for Novo PAC actions, making the program implementable through formal transfer instruments. This matters because it shows concrete follow-through.
- **Source:** Planalto, *Decreto nº 11.855, de 26 de dezembro de 2023*. [Link](https://www.planalto.gov.br/ccivil_03/_ato2023-2026/2023/decreto/d11855.htm)
- **Locator:** Article 1 and Article 2.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Direct executive implementation rule.
- **Contrary evidence:** Transfer rules do not themselves ensure project completion.
- **Cautions:** Procedure, not outcome.
- **Corroboration:** PAC selection pages and ministry implementation notices.
- **Supports:** 8B.2, 8B.5, 8B.6, 8B.8

8. **New PAC was translated into sectoral selection calls**
- **Claim:** The government opened Novo PAC Seleções 2023 with R$65.2 billion for 27 modalities, covering health, education, mobility, sanitation, and housing. This matters because it shows the program moved into specific project selection.
- **Source:** Ministry of Management, 2023-09-27, *Governo Federal lança o Novo PAC Seleções...* [Link](https://www.gov.br/gestao/pt-br/assuntos/noticias/2023/setembro/governo-federal-lanca-o-novo-pac-selecoes-com-apoio-do-ministerio-da-gestao)
- **Locator:** Opening paragraphs on the R$65.2 billion and 27 modalities.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Cross-government execution.
- **Contrary evidence:** Selection calls are not the same as completed works.
- **Cautions:** Program rollout stage.
- **Corroboration:** Casa Civil PAC selections page.
- **Supports:** 8B.2, 8B.3, 8B.5, 8B.6, 8B.10

9. **PAC was tied to social infrastructure**
- **Claim:** The PAC selections included sanitation, daycare, schools, ambulances, CAPS, and other social infrastructure. This matters because it aligns spending with human-development goals rather than purely symbolic investment.
- **Source:** Casa Civil, *Seleções 2023*. [Link](https://www.gov.br/casacivil/pt-br/novopac/selecoes-2023)
- **Locator:** Modalities list on the page.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Formal government planning.
- **Contrary evidence:** The page lists selections, not necessarily deliveries.
- **Cautions:** Pipeline evidence.
- **Corroboration:** Ministry of Management PAC selection announcement.
- **Supports:** 8B.1, 8B.2, 8B.6, 8B.7, 8B.10

10. **Planning was rebuilt through a participatory PPA**
- **Claim:** The PPA 2024–2027 was drafted in 2023 through social participation, plenaries in every state, and a digital platform. This matters because it shows Lula’s government used planning as a real governing instrument.
- **Source:** Ministry of Planning, 2023-07-10, *II Fórum Interconselhos debate o PPA Participativo*. [Link](https://www.gov.br/planejamento/pt-br/assuntos/noticias/2023/julho/ii-forum-interconselhos-debate-ppa-participativo)
- **Locator:** Opening paragraphs on the three forums and statewide plenaries.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Executive planning process.
- **Contrary evidence:** Participation does not itself guarantee policy quality.
- **Cautions:** Process evidence, not outcome evidence.
- **Corroboration:** PPA pages and later sanction notice.
- **Supports:** 8B.1, 8B.2, 8B.3, 8B.5, 8B.6, 8B.10

11. **PPA was framed as the central planning instrument**
- **Claim:** The PPA 2024–2027 was described as the government’s central medium-term planning instrument, with strategic, tactical, and operational layers. This matters because it shows an attempt to restore state planning capacity.
- **Source:** Ministry of Planning PPA 2024–2027 page. [Link](https://www.gov.br/planejamento/pt-br/assuntos/planejamento/plano-plurianual/paginas/paginas-ppa-2024-2027/ppa-2024-2027-english)
- **Locator:** PPA overview and linked report list.
- **Period:** Target year context.
- **Attribution basis:** Formal planning architecture.
- **Contrary evidence:** Planning frameworks can be aspirational if not funded.
- **Cautions:** Official explanatory page.
- **Corroboration:** PPA sanction and participatory process.
- **Supports:** 8B.1, 8B.2, 8B.5, 8B.6, 8B.9

12. **Economic team coordination was explicit**
- **Claim:** Simone Tebet said the economic ministries were coordinated and that the government aimed at better spending quality and fiscal-social balance. This matters because it shows cross-ministerial mobilization rather than isolated bureaucratic action.
- **Source:** Ministry of Planning, 2023-01-13, *Simone Tebet e Esther Dweck destacam integração dos ministérios da área econômica*. [Link](https://www.gov.br/planejamento/pt-br/assuntos/noticias/2023/janeiro/simone-tebet-e-esther-dweck-destacam-integracao-dos-ministerios-da-area-economica)

- **Period:** Target year.

- **Contrary evidence:** Coordination claims need outcome evidence.
- **Cautions:** Official ministry reporting.
- **Corroboration:** Haddad fiscal-framework defense.
- **Supports:** 8B.3, 8B.4, 8B.5, 8B.8

13. **Tebet said 2023 rebuilt policy capacity**
- **Claim:** In December 2023 Tebet said the year was one of recomposing public policies and rebuilding planning. This matters because it is a direct internal assessment of state effectiveness.
- **Source:** Ministry of Planning, 2023-12-21, *Simone Tebet fala sobre os desafios de 2023 e os planos para 2024*. [Link](https://www.gov.br/planejamento/pt-br/assuntos/noticias/2023/dezembro/simone-tebet-fala-sobre-os-desafios-de-2023-e-os-planos-para-2024/)

14. **Haddad defended the fiscal framework as credibility-building**
- **Claim:** Haddad said the new fiscal regime gave certainty that Brazil was moving toward balance. This matters because it shows the team’s program was to restore credibility and lower macro instability.
- **Source:** Ministry of Finance, 2023-08-23, *Novo marco fiscal dá a certeza de que o Brasil caminha para o equilíbrio...* [Link](https://www.gov.br/fazenda/pt-br/assuntos/noticias/2023/agosto/novo-marco-fiscal-da-certeza-de-que-o-brasil-caminha-para-o-equilibrio-diz-ministro-da-fazenda)
- **Locator:** Opening quote and remarks on Congressional approval.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** Key implementer’s public explanation.
- **Contrary evidence:** Credibility claims need market and audit corroboration.
- **Cautions:** Government self-justification.
- **Corroboration:** IMF Article IV and TCU.
- **Supports:** 8B.1, 8B.2, 8B.3, 8B.8, 8B.9

15. **IMF saw a coherent but risk-laden macro program**
- **Claim:** The IMF’s 2023 Article IV said growth was moderating, inflation was falling, and risks were mainly fiscal uncertainty and persistent inflation; it projected 2.1% growth for 2023 and debt stabilization only if measures were implemented. This matters because it is an external check on whether Lula’s program was coherent and credible.
- **Source:** IMF, 2023-07-31, *Brazil: 2023 Article IV Consultation...* [Link](https://www.imf.org/en/publications/cr/issues/2023/07/31/brazil-2023-article-iv-consultation-press-release-staff-report-staff-supplement-and-537328)
- **Locator:** Summary lines on growth, risks, and debt.
- **Period:** Target year.
- **Attribution basis:** External macro assessment.
- **Contrary evidence:** IMF projection is not a verdict on execution quality.
- **Cautions:** Projection, not realized performance.
- **Corroboration:** IBGE and TCU outcomes.
- **Supports:** 8B.3, 8B.6, 8B.7, 8B.9, 8B.10

16. **Labor-market outcomes improved**
- **Claim:** IBGE reported a 2023 annual unemployment rate of 7.8%, down 1.8 percentage points from 2022, with the employed population at a record 100.7 million. This matters because it shows measurable progress toward the government’s growth-and-income objectives.
- **Source:** IBGE, 2024-01-31, *Unemployment rate drops to 7.8% in 2023, lowest figure since 2014*. [Link](https://nada.ibge.gov.br/en/agencia-news/2184-agency-news/news/39035-unemployment-rate-drops-to-7-8-in-2023-lowest-figure-since-2014)
- **Locator:** Summary paragraph.
- **Period:** Later retrospective on 2023.
- **Attribution basis:** Outcome under Lula’s government.
- **Contrary evidence:** Lower unemployment does not eliminate informality or low wages.
- **Cautions:** Retrospective statistics.
- **Corroboration:** Poverty and income releases.
- **Supports:** 8B.7, 8B.10

17. **GDP grew and household demand improved**
- **Claim:** IBGE later reported 2023 GDP growth of 3.2%, with household consumption up 3.2% and government consumption up 3.8%. This matters because it indicates that the government’s growth agenda produced broad macro expansion, even if investment was weaker.
- **Source:** IBGE, 2025-11-06 release on 2023 national accounts. [Link](https://agenciadenoticias.ibge.gov.br/en/agencia-press-room/2185-news-agency/releases-en/44983-gdp-grows-3-2-and-adds-up-to-r-10-9-trillion-in-2023)
- **Locator:** Highlights and household-consumption lines.
- **Period:** Later retrospective on 2023.
- **Attribution basis:** Outcome under Lula’s government.
- **Contrary evidence:** Gross fixed capital formation fell 3%.
- **Cautions:** Late statistical revision/release; aggregate indicator only.
- **Corroboration:** IMF and TCU.
- **Supports:** 8B.7, 8B.10

18. **Poverty and extreme poverty fell**
- **Claim:** IBGE reported poverty fell from 31.6% to 27.4% in 2023, and extreme poverty from 5.9% to 4.4%. This matters because it is the clearest outcome evidence for the government’s social and economic goals.
- **Source:** IBGE, 2024-12-04, *In 2023, poverty in the country drops to lowest level since 2012*. [Link](https://agenciadenoticias.ibge.gov.br/en/agencia-news/2184-news-agency/news/42113-in-2023-poverty-in-the-country-drops-to-lowest-level-since-2012)
- **Locator:** Highlights section.
- **Period:** Later retrospective on 2023.
- **Attribution basis:** Outcome under Lula’s government.
- **Contrary evidence:** Labor recovery and inflation normalization also contributed.
- **Cautions:** Retrospective release.
- **Corroboration:** household-income release and World Bank context.
- **Supports:** 8B.7, 8B.10

19. **Household income and regional imbalance were tracked**
- **Claim:** IBGE’s 2023 per-capita household-income and regional-imbalance releases show the government’s planning apparatus was using distribution-sensitive indicators rather than GDP alone. This matters because effectiveness here requires attention to distribution and regional inequality.
- **Source:** IBGE, *IBGE releases per capita household income 2023...* and *...Coefficient of Regional Imbalance for 2023*. [Links](https://agenciadenoticias.ibge.gov.br/agencia-press-room/2185-news-agency/releases-en/39289-ibge-releases-per-capita-household-income-2023-for-brazil-and-federation-units), [Link](https://agenciadenoticias.ibge.gov.br/agencia-press-room/2185-news-agency/releases-en/40218-ibge-releases-per-capita-household-income-and-coefficient-of-regional-imbalance-for-2023)
- **Locator:** Release titles and summaries.
- **Period:** Later retrospective on 2023.
- **Attribution basis:** Outcome measurement and planning.
- **Contrary evidence:** Measurement is not performance.
- **Cautions:** Data releases only.
- **Corroboration:** IBGE poverty release.
- **Supports:** 8B.5, 8B.7, 8B.10

20. **TCU found both progress and implementation weaknesses**
- **Claim:** The TCU approved Lula’s 2023 accounts with reservations, noting tax-waiver problems, missing health-spending information, and under-documented rural irrigation/agricultural-family spending. This matters because it shows both execution and correction limits.
- **Source:** TCU, 2024-06-12. [Link](https://portal.tcu.gov.br/imprensa/noticias/tcu-aprova-com-ressalvas-as-contas-do-presidente-da-republica-de-2023.htm)
- **Locator:** RESSALVAS and ALERTAS sections.
- **Period:** Later retrospective on 2023.
- **Attribution basis:** Independent audit of government implementation.
- **Contrary evidence:** The accounts were still approved overall.
- **Cautions:** Audit is broad, not program-specific.
- **Corroboration:** IMF and ministry implementation pages.
- **Supports:** 8B.5, 8B.6, 8B.8, 8B.9, 8B.10

**Information environment**
- Strong on formal plans, laws, decrees, and macro statistics.
- Stronger on program creation than on final delivery quality.
- Enough evidence exists to say Lula’s government had a coherent 2023 program and converted much of it into machinery.
- The main uncertainty is durability: many outcomes were positive, but some were still early or dependent on continued execution.

**Questions for deeper research**
- Which New PAC projects actually completed in 2023, rather than merely launched?
- Did the participatory PPA change budget allocations in measurable ways?
- How much of the poverty decline was directly caused by policy versus labor-market recovery?
- Did the fiscal framework materially alter spending composition by year-end?
- Which ministries or agencies showed the strongest execution bottlenecks, and were implementers replaced?

**Promising leads still needing inspection**
- Full PPA 2024–2027 annexes and budget lines.
- Delivery and completion data for New PAC projects in 2023.
- Ministry-level execution reports for housing and health infrastructure.
- Detailed appropriations and outturns for social, housing, and planning programs.
- Independent evaluation of Bolsa Família conditionalities and school/health compliance.

**Source groups**
- **Accepted evidence:** WEB-8B-001 through WEB-8B-019.
- **Corroboration-only sources used in the memo:** TCU accounts review, IMF Article IV, IBGE labor/GDP/poverty releases, PPA pages, PAC pages, and ministry planning/fiscal notices.
- **Uninspected leads:** Full PPA annexes, PAC completion data, ministry execution dashboards, and independent project audits.
- **Rejected sources:** None in this pass.
- **Blocked sources:** None in this pass.

**Source count and remaining gaps**
- **Accepted evidence records:** 19
- **Distinct source families used:** Planalto, ministry notices, Casa Civil, IBGE, IMF, TCU
- **Main remaining gap:** project completion and implementation quality, especially for PAC, housing, and regional distribution of outcomes, not just program launch and legal design.

  "global_findings": [
    "The notebook contains substantial duplicated facts under alternate evidence IDs, especially across chapters; duplicate copies must be excluded from evidence counts.",
    "Several claims rely on snippets, publication pages, metadata, or search-result text rather than precise underlying locators. These are recoverable extraction defects but remain current evidence-quality issues.",
    "Official sources establish laws, decrees, institutional actions, and stated policy, but independent outcome and implementation evidence is uneven.",
    "The local disposition audit reports 24 no-evidence lens gaps, including gaps in Chapters 1B, 3B, 5B, 6B, 7B, and 8B; absence is not adverse evidence.",
    "The strongest continuation blockers are explicit uninspected leads in 3B and 8B, missing personal-integrity evidence in 7B, and missing implementation or distributional evidence in 5B and 6B."
  ],
  "reviewer_summary": "All eight selected chapters contain material research-recoverable gaps. Chapters 1B and 7B are especially weak for their governing methodology: 1B relies heavily on official institutional posture and lacks independent nuclear-safety and implementation checks, while 7B lacks a personal-integrity nexus and includes an invalid negative-evidence synthesis. Chapters 3B, 4B, 5B, 6B, and 8B contain useful evidence but require fuller attribution, contrary evidence, or outcome and implementation coverage. Chapter 2B is comparatively stronger for documented diplomatic and humanitarian actions, but remains weaker for independent effectiveness and adverse-position checks. Continuation is warranted across the selected scope; no chapter is sufficiently complete to pass solely on source count."
}

--- RESEARCH CONTINUATION ROUND 1 ---

1B review round 1 is still open. I expanded the evidence pool with direct source-family and adverse searches, but I could not update `research-ledger-manifest.json` from this session because the local shell is blocked by the sandbox wrapper. I’m therefore giving you a manifest-ready delta: new source-claim units, improved locators, and the dedupe/correction list.

**What I added**

1. **ABACC 2023 verification activity, training, and IAEA coordination**
- Source: ABACC, *ABACC at the 67th IAEA General Conference*.
- URL: https://www.abacc.org.br/en/news/abacc-67th-iaea-general-conference/
- Locator: lines 63-66.
- Claim: ABACC said it was carrying out verification activities in 2023, modernizing measurement/containment/surveillance systems, and coordinating with the IAEA on inspector training and cooperation.
- Why it helps: this is a more precise safeguards-implementation source than the prior self-descriptive nuclear-sector page.
- Limits: still ABACC self-reporting, not an external audit.
- Suggested new record ID: `WEB-1B-013`
- Suggested lenses: `1B.3`, `1B.4`, `1B.7`, `1B.10`

2. **ABACC inspection procedures and coverage**
- Source: ABACC, *Inspections*.
- URL: https://www.abacc.org.br/en/the-abacc/procedures/
- Locator: the “Inspections” section and the table of facilities under safeguards.
- Claim: ABACC and IAEA inspections are joint, cross-checked, and cover Brazilian safeguarded facilities through a structured pre-inspection / in-situ / post-inspection process.
- Why it helps: this grounds the safeguards system operationally instead of just institutionally.
- Limits: procedure, not outcome.
- Suggested new record ID: `WEB-1B-014`
- Suggested lenses: `1B.3`, `1B.4`, `1B.7`

3. **ABACC regional safeguards architecture**
- Source: ABACC, *SCCC*.
- URL: https://www.abacc.org.br/en/the-abacc/sccc/
- Locator: lines about reporting abnormalities, inspection scope, containment/surveillance, and periodic reporting to national authorities and the IAEA.
- Claim: the regional system formally requires ABACC to report safeguards abnormalities and to use accounting, transfers, and surveillance controls.
- Why it helps: stronger formal architecture evidence for the safeguards chain.
- Limits: still architecture, not performance.
- Suggested new record ID: `WEB-1B-015`
- Suggested lenses: `1B.3`, `1B.4`, `1B.7`

4. **Emergency drill showed actual response machinery**
- Source: CNEN / IEN, *CNEN atua no Exercício Itaorna 2023 para avaliar capacidade de resposta a emergências da Central Nuclear*.
- URL: https://www.gov.br/ien/pt-br/assuntos/noticias/cnen-atua-no-exercicio-itaorna-2023-para-avaliar-capacidade-de-resposta-a-emergencias-da-central-nuclear
- Locator: lines 287-291 from the extracted passage.
- Claim: roughly 60 federal/state/municipal entities and about 1,200 professionals participated in a nuclear emergency/security exercise coordinated by GSI/PR, with evacuation, sirens, iodine distribution, and radiological monitoring.
- Why it helps: this is the best implementation evidence I found for emergency-response readiness.
- Limits: simulated exercise, not real incident performance.
- Suggested new record ID: `WEB-1B-016`
- Suggested lenses: `1B.3`, `1B.8`, `1B.10`

5. **Angra 1 incident handling and regulatory follow-through**
- Source: CNEN, *Nota de esclarecimento sobre vazamento na usina de Angra*.
- URL: https://www.gov.br/cnen/pt-br/assunto/ultimas-noticias/nota-de-esclarecimento-sobre-vazamento-na-usina-de-angra
- Locator: lines 259-264.
- Claim: CNEN said its resident team inspected the Angra release, sent three inspection teams, found a maximum release of 90 liters, and concluded the detected radionuclides posed no risk to the population or environment.
- Why it helps: this is direct safety-incident handling evidence and a useful contrast to official “all is well” rhetoric.
- Limits: CNEN is the regulator and operator-facing authority, so it is not fully independent.
- Suggested new record ID: `WEB-1B-017`
- Suggested lenses: `1B.3`, `1B.8`, `1B.10`

6. **Adverse / contrary evidence: Angra 1 delayed reporting and legal scrutiny**
- Source: Reuters via UOL, *Eletronuclear é autuada após vazamento de água radioativa de Angra 1*.
- URL: https://economia.uol.com.br/noticias/reuters/2023/03/23/eletronuclear-e-autuada-apos-vazamento-de-agua-radioativa-de-angra-1.htm
- Locator: lines 514-522 from the extracted passage.
- Claim: Reuters reported Eletronuclear allegedly took 21 days to notify Ibama and CNEN; the MPF said there were signs of concealment and the federal court ordered a full assessment of contamination and impacts.
- Why it helps: this directly addresses the reviewer’s request for adverse/contrary evidence on safety performance and transparency.
- Limits: the report includes allegations from the MPF and the company’s defense, so it should be retained as a contested incident record.
- Suggested new record ID: `WEB-1B-018`
- Suggested lenses: `1B.3`, `1B.8`, `1B.10`

7. **Adverse / contrary evidence: INB economic fragility**
- Source: TCU, *Indústrias Nucleares do Brasil apresentam fragilidade econômica*.
- URL: https://portal.tcu.gov.br/imprensa/noticias/industrias-nucleares-do-brasil-apresentam-fragilidade-economica
- Locator: lines 20-30.
- Claim: the TCU found reduced revenue margins, lack of regulatory incentives, no institutionalized commercial policy, and incomplete risk-management implementation at INB.
- Why it helps: this is the strongest counterweight to a simple “Brazil’s nuclear machinery is safe and effective” story.
- Limits: this is about the state-owned fuel-cycle company, not the whole nuclear system.
- Suggested new record ID: `WEB-1B-019`
- Suggested lenses: `1B.3`, `1B.4`, `1B.7`, `1B.10`

8. **Legal / institutional baseline from IAEA country facts**
- Source: IAEA Office of Legal Affairs country factsheet for Brazil.
- URL: https://ola.iaea.org/Applications/FactSheets/Country/Detail?code=BR
- Locator: safeguards agreements table showing the Brazil-Argentina-ABACC-IAEA safeguards agreement in force.
- Claim: Brazil remains inside the formal ABACC/IAEA safeguards regime and under a comprehensive safeguards framework.
- Why it helps: cleaner treaty baseline than the prior generic ministry description.
- Limits: legal status, not performance.
- Suggested new record ID: `WEB-1B-020`
- Suggested lenses: `1B.4`, `1B.6`, `1B.10`

9. **Durable operational context for the submarine program**
- Source: Casa Civil, *Inovação para a Indústria da Defesa*.
- URL: https://www.gov.br/casacivil/pt-br/novopac-antiga/novopac-backup-8-07-2024/inovacao-para-a-industria-da-defesa
- Locator: section noting advanced execution of two conventional submarines and the nuclear-propulsion submarine funding/2023-2026 horizon.
- Claim: the PROSUB line remained funded and operational in 2023, with advanced work on conventional submarines and continued nuclear-propulsion spending.
- Why it helps: this is better execution evidence than the older generic defense-sector page.
- Limits: still largely government self-reporting.
- Suggested new record ID: `WEB-1B-021`
- Suggested lenses: `1B.1`, `1B.3`, `1B.8`, `1B.10`

**What now looks better supported**
- The 2023 safeguards story is no longer just “Brazil says it has peaceful nuclear energy.”
- There is now direct evidence of:
- ongoing verification work,
- inspector training and modernization,
- routine emergency-response exercises,
- a real operational incident at Angra,
- and an adverse audit on the fuel-cycle company.

**What remains weak**
- Independent, non-governmental evaluation of safeguards performance in 2023.
- Independent evidence on whether the emergency drill translated into better real-world response.
- Independent proof of whether the Angra incident was fully and transparently managed end-to-end.
- Any parliamentary or whistleblower material on nuclear oversight.
- Any direct evidence on arms-restriction effectiveness as a catastrophic-risk measure.

**Duplicate / dedupe corrections**
- The prior ledger should collapse the overlapping 1B “peaceful nuclear posture” items into a single baseline fact instead of counting alternate copies separately.
- The prior ledger should treat the following as distinct underlying facts, not duplicates:
- `WEB-1B-013` verification activity / inspector modernization
- `WEB-1B-016` emergency drill
- `WEB-1B-017` Angra inspection handling
- `WEB-1B-018` delayed reporting / concealment allegation
- `WEB-1B-019` INB fragility
- `WEB-1B-020` treaty/safeguards baseline
- `WEB-1B-021` PROSUB execution status

- I could not write `research-ledger-manifest.json` in this turn because the local shell is blocked.

--- SUPERVISOR RESEARCH TAKEOVER ROUND 2 ---

Completed a bounded continuation of Chapter 1B. No score assigned.

Added/recovered nine defensible source-claim units:

- `WEB-1B-013`: ABACC 2023 safeguards verification: 108 inspections, 59 Brazilian facility visits, no evidence of non-compliance, and 81 inspectors trained. [ABACC Annual Report 2023](https://www.abacc.org.br/en/wp-content/uploads/sites/2/2024/08/Annual-Report-2023.pdf), pp. 3–6.
- `WEB-1B-014`: Brazil-specific ABACC activity included 59 inspections, 21 design-information visits, 14 unannounced inspections, and 181 inspector-days. Same report, pp. 12–14.
- `WEB-1B-015`: ABACC/IAEA coordination used shared surveillance, measurement systems, technical meetings, and joint safeguards procedures. Same report, pp. 14–16.
- `WEB-1B-016`: Itaorna 2023 emergency exercise involved approximately 60 institutions and 1,200 professionals, testing evacuation, radiological monitoring, emergency command, and medical response. [CNEN/IEN](https://www.gov.br/ien/pt-br/assuntos/noticias/cnen-atua-no-exercicio-itaorna-2023-para-avaliar-capacidade-de-resposta-a-emergencias-da-central-nuclear), lines 287–303.
- `WEB-1B-017`: A November 2023 Angra 1 electrical-loss event temporarily affected cooling to the spent-fuel pool; emergency cooling was restored within five minutes and CNEN monitored the investigation. [CNEN notice](https://www.gov.br/cnen/pt-br/assunto/ultimas-noticias/nota-da-cnen-sobre-um-evento-nao-usual-enu-na-central-nuclear-almirante-alvaro-alberto), lines 255–275.
- `WEB-1B-018`: Reuters reported that Eletronuclear took 21 days to notify Ibama and CNEN about a low-level radioactive-water release; prosecutors alleged concealment, while the company disputed the characterization. [Reuters/UOL](https://economia.uol.com.br/noticias/reuters/2023/03/23/eletronuclear-e-autuada-apos-vazamento-de-agua-radioativa-de-angra-1.htm), lines 514–524.
- `WEB-1B-019`: TCU found INB had weak financial resilience, incomplete risk-management implementation, inadequate commercial policy, and regulatory gaps affecting nuclear-fuel efficiency. [TCU](https://portal.tcu.gov.br/imprensa/noticias/industrias-nucleares-do-brasil-apresentam-fragilidade-economica), lines 19–31.
- `WEB-1B-020`: ABACC’s inspection architecture uses joint ABACC/IAEA inspections, reciprocal inspectors, three inspection stages, and coverage of 26 Brazilian safeguarded facilities. [ABACC procedures](https://www.abacc.org.br/en/the-abacc/procedures/), lines 71–104.
- `WEB-1B-021`: Brazil’s 2023 firearms recadastration reached 939,154 weapons, providing implementation evidence for Lula’s arms-control reversal. [Ministry of Justice](https://www.gov.br/mj/pt-br/assuntos/noticias/mjsp-atinge-99-do-recadastramento-de-armas-de-fogo).

The evidence now supports a more balanced 1B record: safeguards and emergency-response machinery were active and operationally tested, but Angra reporting raised a concrete transparency concern and TCU identified structural weaknesses in the nuclear-fuel enterprise. Arms restrictions show measurable administrative implementation, though not yet proven effects on violence or catastrophic risk.
